Finding-by-finding: (1) trainer consumes positive-clip dir + bundled negative features (~30,000 hours pre-computed) + bundled MIT RIRs + bundled background audio, configured via one YAML; default target acceptance is recall ≥ 0.5 / FA ≤ 0.2/hr / accuracy ≥ 0.7; practical floor ~3,000–4,000 positives, default ~10,000+2,000; (2) current upstream practice consolidates around `piper-sample-generator` (PyPI claims OS-Independent but depends on piper-phonemize which has no Windows wheels), so the predecessor's hand-rolled 200-speaker × prosody loop can be replaced with a ~30-line wrapper around `python -m piper_sample_generator`; verifier-models on Eric's voice are a documented but out-of-MVP follow-up; (3) trainer outputs `my_custom_model/<name>.onnx` + `.tflite`, the runtime scaffold at `wake_word.py:104-116` already accepts an `.onnx` path verbatim, model size ~200 KB–5 MB git-trackable, drop point should be the QUEUE-P2.2 path `sabrina-2/models/openwakeword/hey_sabrina.onnx` (NOT the predecessor plan's `voices/wake/hey_sabrina.onnx`); (4) Windows validation procedure maps cleanly onto QUEUE P2.3 — pre-flight + threshold pick via `sabrina wake-test` + 5/5 true-positive smoke + 5-min podcast false-positive smoke + first-audio latency check; (5) **load-bearing finding: the predecessor plan's training-on-Windows-CPU assumption is wrong against current upstream tooling. Recommend Path B (run upstream pipeline inside WSL2; Eric has Ubuntu + Ubuntu-22.04 installed) as canonical with Path A (manual Piper-binary on Windows; predecessor's current path) as fallback and Path C (Colab) as last resort.** Recommendation = **Go**: predecessor plan is fundamentally sound, just needs the WSL2 amendment.
- Surprises: (a) The piper-phonemize Windows-build gap is the single biggest divergence from the predecessor plan; the plan would have shipped a manual Piper-binary script and missed the upstream-canonical piper-sample-generator path entirely. (b) openWakeWord's verifier-model feature is exactly the missing high-leverage tool for "low false-accept rate without retraining the base model" — predecessor plan scoped it out as MVP; worth keeping it as a documented follow-up. (c) Issue #110 + discussion #62 + #45 all confirm that the "automatic" notebook is brittle in practice — running outside Colab on a real WSL2 box is the better-tested path. (d) The runtime scaffold landed 2026-04-25 already accepts a custom `.onnx` path; **no runtime code change is needed for P2.2 ship — only a config file change in `sabrina.toml`**. (e) `tools/uv.environments = ["sys_platform == 'win32'"]` already declares the project Windows-only at resolve-time, with a comment explicitly mentioning openwakeword 0.6's Linux tflite-runtime branch — that decision is in-tree, just not documented as a decision doc.

Resolution rules per the migration-audit intent: (1) None or empty/whitespace-only string → return None for sounddevice's default; (2) int or all-digit string → that exact index after channel-validation, raises `ValueError` if out-of-range or output-only; (3) non-digit string → case-insensitive substring match on input-capable device names, first-match wins; (4) no name match → log warning + fall back to system default; (5) default unavailable or output-only → `RuntimeError` with the failing requested string in the message. Authored `sabrina-2/tests/test_audio_utils.py` (187 lines, 23 tests across 5 cases: 3 defer-to-default (None/empty/whitespace), 7 explicit int/digit (int / digit-str / whitespace-around-digit / out-of-range / negative / output-only-int / output-only-digit-str), 5 name-substring (exact / partial / case-insensitive 2x / first-match-wins / skips-output-only-fallback), 4 no-match-fallback (default / default-None / default-out-of-range / default-output-only), 4 defensive shapes (missing max_input_channels / non-int max_input_channels / empty device list with no match / int-against-empty-list)).
